## Agent report — 2026-10-25T18:21:58.057Z

**Report from an agent of this session:**


  # Design review, round 2: entitlement-agent
  
  I read the proposal, every data model except the draft, all ADRs, the ER/sequence/activity diagrams, production-readiness, AI_AS_ARCHITECT/DEVELOPER, constitution §4, and grepped both feature specs. I modified no files. Items are numbered across sections, most important first. Paths are relative to `tech-docs/` unless noted.
  
  ## Must fix (before the next feature spec)
  
  **1. System actors cannot pass the AU-4 gate, and AC-6 blocks system cleanup on suspended accounts.**
  - `adr/authorization.md` AU-4 says: "every use case that touches a subject account asks for `account.read`".
  - `data-model/access.md` §2 lists `account | read | Employee`. Composite foreign keys stop a System role from holding it. `entitlement-maintenance` holds only `grant.record-expiry` and `change-request.apply`.
  - So `sys_approval_process` fails the gate on every apply (SU-11) and every `approval.expire`. `sys_grant_expiry` fails it on record-expiry.
  - Separately, the AC-6 amendment says a suspended subject "cannot be the subject of feature actions or changes". That denies grant-expiry recording, approval expiry and case-close cancellations on suspended accounts.
  - **Fix:** limit AU-4 to employee-initiated use cases. Define a class of "terminating" actions (end, expire, cancel: never add anything) that skips the subject-suspension stop. Put both rules in AU-4/AC-6 and the activity diagram.
  
  **2. Lifecycle action keys can never be allowed.**
  - Proposal §4.3 says "Authorizing an action checks the permission with the same key". AU-3 says the access step "denies unless a role or assignment explicitly grants the permission".
  - `case.close`, `case.set-subject`, `case.set-contact`, `case.authenticate-contact`, `case.reassign`, `change-request.confirm`, `change-request.cancel` and `approval.expire` are "action keys only" (`data-model/attribution.md` §2, SU-9). Each is either always denied or skips the chain, and neither is specified.
  - **Fix:** give each lifecycle key a governing permission plus a rule. For example: `case.*` → `case.open` plus "is assignee"; confirm/cancel → the change kind's employee permission; `case.reassign` → a new `case.reassign` permission for supervisors; `approval.expire` → `change-request.apply`.
  - Also, `case.reassign` is missing from SU-9's list and from AT-5's amendment, but it is in `data-model/support.md` §3.
  
  **3. The case-assignee check is not in the decision path.**
  - S05 checks the assignee only at connection time. AI_AS_DEVELOPER §2.10 says "the assignment is re-checked per message", but no handler, PIP attribute or reason code exists for it.
  - Confirm/cancel are endpoints "or a typed frame" (SU-9). A reassigned CSR, or any CSR holding the permission, could confirm, close or set the subject on someone else's case.
  - **Fix:** add `context.actor_is_assignee`, enforced by the Support PEP template or a chain handler. Add reason `support.not-case-assignee`.
  
  **4. ChangePlan coverage has no limit, so courtesy and trial limits can be bypassed.**
  - `data-model/support.md` §5: ChangePlan takes "`source` (`Trial`, `Courtesy`) and `duration_days`" for a Paid target.
  - `courtesy-up-to-14-days` governs only `subscription.add-courtesy-term`. A CSR can upgrade Initech to Business with a 3650-day Trial and need no approval (rank 3 with a contact). They can also re-"change" pro→pro with a Courtesy term.
  - A ChangePlan on a lapsed subscription (Globex) restores access for free. `data-model/entitlement.md` §5 does not say which term is cut when none covers now.
  - **Fix:**
    - add PIP attributes `context.coverage_source` and `context.duration_days` for `subscription.change-plan`
    - add a policy such as `trial-coverage-up-to-14-days`, and make the courtesy limit govern both actions
    - require target ≠ current plan
    - define ChangePlan without a covering term: refuse with `entitlement.no-covering-term`, or treat it as a courtesy term.
  
  **5. A Conditional confirm bumps the version, and approved changes skip the staleness check.**
  - `data-model/support.md` §4 step 1 runs `UPDATE account SET version = version + 1 … AND version = $2` before the decision. On `Conditional`, the transaction commits AwaitingApproval plus the approval. The account version has then moved with no change, which makes every other pending request stale.
  - At apply, SU-11 says "the entitlement diff is recomputed against the current state". That applies a diff neither the CSR nor the approver saw, which contradicts SU-4 ("nothing different from what was displayed is ever applied").
  - **Fix:**
    - on Conditional, lock and compare without incrementing
    - at apply after approval, re-check the stored diff's `test` operations (or the version); on failure, end `Canceled` with `support.stale-change-request`
    - bump the version only at apply.
  
  **6. Whose decision is re-run, and self-approval through reassignment.**
  - SU-11 re-runs "the requester's original decision". `no-self-approval` compares only `context.requester_account_id`.
  - Requester and confirmer can differ after a reassignment (`requested_by` ≠ `confirmed_by`, `data-model/support.md` §4). The confirmer is the performer whose decision went `Conditional`, and a supervisor-confirmer can approve their own confirmed request.
  - **Fix:** re-run the confirmer's decision. Add `context.confirmer_account_id`. `no-self-approval` should require the approver to be neither `requested_by` nor `confirmed_by`.
  
  **7. Contact authentication is not bound to the contact.**
  - `support_case` holds `contact_authenticated_at/kind/by` beside a `contact_account_id` that `case.set-contact` can change; only the subject is "set once".
  - If A is authenticated and the contact is then set to B, B is treated as authenticated, so SU-8/SU-10 can be bypassed.
  - **Fix:** `case.set-contact` clears the authentication columns, or store `contact_authenticated_account_id` and have the PIP compare it with the contact. Add a verification test.
  
  **8. A suspended contact can still ask for changes.**
  - AU-1 says the account step checks "neither the actor's nor the subject's account". The activity diagram checks "A, or S". A contact's account with an `OperationalSuspension` (fraud) or `NoActiveMembership` hold still passes contact access.
  - **Fix:** the account handler also checks the contact, reporting `account.<hold>` with the fact "contact".
  
  **9. SU-13's own-transaction audit loses the status change.**
  - SU-13: "a refused confirmation rolls back, and its audit record is then written in its own transaction". The `Refused`/`Canceled` status rolls back with it. S16a needs `Canceled`, and S16 needs `Refused`.
  - **Fix:** the second transaction writes the status and the audit together, guarded by `WHERE change_status = 'AwaitingConfirmation'` so a concurrent confirm cannot be overwritten.
  
  **10. External side effects inside DB transactions have no owner.**
  - `change_approval.process_instance_id` is NOT NULL, so the Workflow instance must exist before commit. If the transaction rolls back, the instance is orphaned.
  - `case.close` "cancels … their process instances" inside one transaction (`data-model/support.md` §3 and §8). Case notifications go to the Agent DO.
  - **Fix:** use a deterministic instance id (= `approval_id`) and create it idempotently after commit, with a reconciliation sweep or a small outbox. Instances re-read DB status before acting. Close commits first, then terminates on a best-effort basis.
  
  **11. The Approved → apply window has no defined behavior.**
  - Close cancels requests "awaiting approval", but the approval may already be `Approved` while the workflow applies. The `approval_status` and `change_status` transitions for that window are undefined.
  - **Fix:**
    - apply locks the `change_request` row and requires `AwaitingApproval` + `Approved`
    - decide requires `Pending` and `now < expires_at`
    - close cancels only `Pending` approvals; state what happens to Approved-but-unapplied requests (recommend: close waits for or refuses while one is applying).
  
  **12. Reason-code register gaps** (`data-model/attribution.md` §4a):
  - Missing codes:
    - `support.approval-denied`, `support.approval-expired`
    - `support.case-closed` (for cancel-on-close, and for actions on a closed case)
    - `support.contact-authentication-failed` (failed attempts "are audit records" but have no code)
    - `support.not-case-assignee`
    - ChangePlan validation codes (`entitlement.plan-unchanged`, plan not in the ladder, not `Standalone`), or a stated rule that these are validation errors rather than decisions.
  - AT-5's 2026-10-24 amendment still says "`policy_set_version` references `policy_set`", which contradicts CM-15's soft reference.
  
  **13. Cancel subscription and a successful downgrade have no scenario.**
  - Proposal §1 promises "upgrade, downgrade, cancel". The kind `CancelSubscription`, its two permissions and `contact-is-authenticated` exist, but S14–S20 cover only an upgrade and refusals.
  - **Fix:** add S14 cases for a downgrade and a cancel, defining the ends (terms, add-ons and grants become `SubscriptionCanceled`, and the explanation reason `entitlement.subscription-canceled`). Otherwise mark cancel "designed, not built".
  
  **14. How many decisions and audit records per use case?**
  - The AU-4 gate plus the action decision, plus the "direct apply recorded under the governing permission, `Succeeded`" (SU-9), plus the change-request.confirm decision could be up to 3–4 records, each taking the global head lock.
  - The explain-access spec audits reads only as `account.read` (D3), which is fine for reads. Changes are unspecified, and S20 history depends on the answer.
  - **Fix:** define the canonical record set per use case. Recommendation: the gate becomes a finding of the action's decision, with one record per decision plus one `Succeeded`/`Failed` per execution.
  
  **15. Read tools take any account; the case has one subject.**
  - `features/explain-access/explain-access.verification-spec.md` line 725 says the tools "take the account explicitly". SU-2 says the subject is "the account context of every question in it".
  - **Decide before case-lifecycle:** once the subject is set, tools act only on it, and only `findCustomerAccount` crosses accounts. Specify what `case_id` an off-subject read records.
  
  ## Should fix / decide
  
  **16. The Enterprise read policy disappears when coverage lapses.**
  - `enterprise-accounts-need-level-2` applies when `has(resource.plan_key) && resource.plan_key == 'enterprise'`. `plan_key` comes from the covering term, so a lapsed or ended Enterprise contract becomes readable by level 1.
  - **Fix:** classify by a stable attribute: the last term's plan, or an account or customer segment.
  
  **17. When a trial ends, the account has nothing.**
  - Initech Free → Pro `Trial`: when the trial ends (EN-10 "nothing works unless a term covers now"), Initech loses even its free features.
  - **Decide:** fall back to a `FreePlan` term at `scheduled_end_at` for the demo, or accept this and show it in the explanation.
  
  **18. Future-dated terms and term end reasons.**
  - "Monthly renewal: a new pro Payment term starts where the last one ends." If billing pre-creates such terms, a plan change overlaps them, yet "terms never overlap".
  - **Decide:** forbid future terms in the demo, or have a plan change void them (`end_reason` such as `Superseded`).
  - Also, a subscription ending with `CustomerRemoved` leaves terms and grants with only `SubscriptionCanceled`. Consider `SubscriptionEnded`.
  
  **19. Changes on the CSR's own initiative skip SU-8.**
  - An empty `contact_account_id` means no contact checks. A CSR can cancel or downgrade with nobody asking; only `upgrade-beyond-pro-needs-contact-or-approval` guards this.
  - Also unclear whether `context.has_contact` comes from the change request or the case.
  - **Decide:** add `cancel-needs-contact-or-approval` (and possibly a downgrade equivalent), and fix where `has_contact` comes from.
  
  **20. Grant size is unbounded, and the seat check uses the plan's quota only.**
  - `grants-up-to-14-days` limits duration, not quantity: a 1,000,000-seat grant is allowed.
  - `downgrade-fits-seats` compares against `context.target_seat_quota`, but add-ons and seat grants also count.
  - **Fix:** add `context.quota` and `context.feature_key`, and define the target quota as the target's effective seats.
  
  **21. Session renewal does not re-check status.**
  - The renewal block in `diagrams/sign-in.sequence.puml` re-issues without checking identity status or account holds. A terminated employee keeps renewing up to 8 h with a live WebSocket. Decisions still fail, but `/api/me` and connections keep working.
  - **Fix:** renewal loads the principal and refuses with `account.internal-identity-inactive` or the hold code. Also state behavior before half-life (204 without a cookie?).
  - sid/jti is now consistent: CM-16, AT-6 amendment, attribution model and ER, sign-in spec. AT-6's Decision text still says `jti`, which the amendment overrides.
  
  **22. ListPendingApprovals and DecideApproval are not gated.**
  - Both touch subjects, but SU-12 does not mention the AU-4 gate.
  - **Fix:** gate each listed approval like lookup candidates, and audit the panel read.
  
  **23. Stale text and contradictions left over:**
  - `diagrams/support.er.puml` still has `AddCourtesyPeriod`; the model says `AddCourtesyTerm`.
  - The `adr/entitlement.md` Purpose still says "access periods". AU-2 still says "obligations".
  - `diagrams/data-model.er.puml` is the superseded draft (`Trialing`, `is_paid`, `caller`) but is not labeled as such.
  - `production-readiness.md` §7 still says "Contact verification methods".
  - CM-13 and `data-model/README.md` say "Every `*_by` column references `account`", but CM-15 forbids that in the identity subsystems below Account. Add "except below Account (soft)".
  - Proposal §7 says each feature is "deployable on its own". In fact change-subscription needs case-lifecycle (subject, contact authentication), and grants-and-approvals needs change-subscription's confirmation machinery. State the order.
  
  ## Production notes to add
  
  **24. Contact authentication freshness.** It is recorded once per case and still trusted at apply up to 72 h later. It is a CSR's claim with no evidence attached. There is no step-up for high-risk changes (cancel, Enterprise).
  
  **25. Workflow ↔ DB reconciliation.** Orphaned or missing process instances, timers lost through a crash between commit and instance creation, and notifications to an offline case agent.
  
  **26. The global head lock spans network round trips.** Under item 14, every read and change holds `audit_chain_head` across Worker↔Hyperdrive↔PostgreSQL round trips until commit. This makes it a hot row, beyond the current "audit cost on the read path" entry. Approval panels and lookups multiply the writes.
  
  **27. Session end.** Sign-out and credential replacement do not invalidate tokens (up to 30 min). There is no per-`sid` deny list. Also, nobody ends a session on an identity status change.
  
  ## Modularity assessment
  
  **28 (must fix). Use cases as PEPs in lower layers create cycles.**
  - Layer order (CM-15, AI_AS_ARCHITECT §1): identities → account → access, product → entitlement → policy → authorization → support. A use case may import only lower public use cases.
  - These lower-layer use cases need to call higher subsystems:
  
  | Use case | Subsystem | Must call |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | RecordGrantExpiry | Entitlement | Authorization |
  | place/release hold | Account | Authorization |
  | role-assignment (S29) | Access | Authorization |
  | Membership end (ends assignments, places `NoActiveMembership`) | Customer Identity | Access, Account |
  | Customer creation (creates the account) | Customer Identity | Account |
  | Identity status (places holds) | Internal Identity | Account |
  | SignIn (reads `account_status`, binds the account; sign-in diagram) | Internal Identity | Account |
  
  - **Fix:**
    - reach Authorization and Attribution through shared ports (`port/AuthorizationDecider`, `port/AuditRecorder`), wired at bootstrap
    - move each cascade to the highest subsystem it touches, or to an application-level use case
    - move SignIn into Account (or the application).
  
  **29. Attribution is called a sink but has foreign keys up to Account.**
  - CM-15 says attribution "depends on no one's tables". `audit_chain_record.actor_account_id` and `subject_account_id`, and `audit_participant.account_id`, are foreign keys to `account`.
  - In code, every subsystem (including Internal Identity's SignIn) writes to it, which needs it below them. Its schema puts it above Account.
  - **Fix:** make the account references soft (consistent with the CM-15 rationale "never blocked by another subsystem's table"), or state attribution's real position plus a shared port.
  
  **30. Owners still to define:**
  - **PIP context:** `data-model/policy.md` §3 says "the PIP sets `context.approval_granted`", but Authorization cannot read Support's tables (AU-5 says the use case passes the context). Authorization should own the typed context contract per action, and the PEP fill it.
  - **Action-key registry:** Policy's build validates "known action keys", but lifecycle keys belong to Support, which sits above Policy. Own the full key list low (Access, next to `resource_operation`).
  - **`account.version` bumps:** "changes on every write that affects the account" covers Entitlement (billing terms, cancel, expiry?), Account (holds) and Support (apply). Use one Account operation called inside the UnitOfWork, with a list of the writes that bump. Otherwise billing changes slip past the staleness check.
  - **Cross-subsystem transactions:** apply is owned by Support. Close (Support + Workflows) and cancel/customer removal (Entitlement → Support pending requests) have no owner.
  
  ## Overall
  
  1. The last round fixed the big structural issues: one term timeline, identity-only tokens, a single read gate, soft references, and contact authentication.
  2. The new rules collide with each other at the edges. The read gate and the suspension stop block the system actors. Lifecycle keys cannot get past default deny. ChangePlan coverage gets around the courtesy limit.
  3. The change-request state machine needs a precise transaction spec: the Conditional version bump, re-checking the stored diff at apply, persisting refusals, and the races between approval, close and expiry.
  4. Modularity is close. Making Authorization and Attribution shared ports and moving the identity cascades upward removes the remaining cycles.
  5. Fix items 1–15 and 28 before writing the case-lifecycle and change-subscription specs; the rest are decisions or notes.

## Overall verdict

- **Documents are in good shape for a human reader:** no broken links, tables or flow-style YAML, and the front matter is everywhere except one References row.
- **The scenarios are correct in outcome,** apart from one rule contradiction (D1 below). Most of the remaining spec findings are Givens that don't yet state every fact they rely on.
- **The design is structurally sound,** but last round's new rules **collide at the edges.** Three examples:
  - the read gate blocks the system accounts
  - lifecycle actions can never pass default deny
  - a plan change can grant unlimited free coverage.

  The change-request state machine also needs a precise transaction specification.

## A. Decisions for you, with my recommendation

**Contradictions and gaps that block the next feature specs:**

| # | Issue | Recommendation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D1 | **A lookup whose only candidate is denied.** The rule says denied candidates are dropped (giving `NotFound`), but S06 and S08 expect `Denied`. | **If every candidate is denied, the result is `Denied` with their reason codes; otherwise denied candidates are dropped.** The CSR learns why, without leaking anything they weren't already denied. |
| D2 | **System accounts can't pass the `account.read` gate** (only employees hold it), and AC-6 stops system cleanup on suspended accounts (grant expiry, approval timeout, closing a case). | The gate applies to **employee-initiated** use cases. Define **terminating actions** (end, expire, cancel: they never add anything); they bypass the subject-suspension stop. |
| D3 | **Lifecycle actions can never be allowed:** default deny needs a permission, and they have none. | Each lifecycle key gets a **governing permission**: `case.*` → `case.open` plus "is the assignee"; confirm and cancel → the change kind's permission; `case.reassign` → a new permission for supervisors; `approval.expire` → `change-request.apply`. |
| D4 | **The assignee isn't checked when deciding:** only at connection. A reassigned CSR could still confirm or close. | A PIP attribute `context.actor_is_assignee`, checked by Support's PEP template; reason `support.not-case-assignee`. |
| D5 | **A plan change's coverage has no limit.** An upgrade could carry a 3,650-day `Trial`, or a pro→pro "change" could hand out free coverage. A plan change on a lapsed subscription restores access for free. | Coverage limits apply to plan changes too (the 14-day courtesy rule, plus a trial limit). The target must differ from the current plan. A plan change **without a covering term is refused** (`entitlement.no-covering-term`); restoring coverage is a separate courtesy term. |
| D6 | **The version check, revisited.** A `Conditional` confirmation bumps the account version without changing anything, which makes every other pending request stale. And recomputing the diff after approval applies something the approver never saw, which contradicts SU-4. | Bump the version **only when applying**. At apply after approval, re-check the **stored** diff's `test` operations; if they fail, the change is `Canceled` as stale. **This reverses the "recompute the diff" part of R1**, because it conflicts with "apply exactly what was shown". |
| D7 | **Whose decision is re-run, and self-approval through reassignment.** | Re-run the **confirmer's** decision. `no-self-approval` excludes both the requester and the confirmer. |
| D8 | **Contact authentication isn't tied to the contact:** authenticate A, switch the contact to B, and B counts as authenticated. | Changing the contact **clears the authentication columns**. |
| D9 | **A suspended contact can still ask for changes.** | The account step also checks the contact's account. |
| D10 | **SU-13's separate audit transaction loses the status:** the `Refused` or `Canceled` status rolls back with the refused transaction. | The second transaction writes the status **and** the audit record together, guarded by "still `AwaitingConfirmation`". |
| D11 | **Workflow side effects inside database transactions:** an instance can be orphaned by a rollback. | The instance id is the approval id, created after commit (an outbox with a reconciliation sweep). Instances re-read the database before acting. Closing a case commits first, then terminates instances on a best-effort basis. |
| D12 | **The window between approval and apply has no rules.** | Apply locks the request and requires `Approved` + `AwaitingApproval`. A decision requires `Pending` and the deadline not passed. Closing a case cancels only `Pending` approvals and refuses while an apply is in progress. |
| D13 | **How many audit records per use case?** The gate, the decision and the execution could each write one. | **The gate becomes one finding within the action's decision.** That gives one record per decision, plus one `Succeeded` or `Failed` per execution. |
| D14 | **Read tools take any account,** yet a case has one subject. | Once the subject is set, the read tools act only on it; only `findCustomerAccount` crosses accounts. |
| D15 | **Cancel and a successful downgrade have no scenario.** | Add both to change-subscription, defining what ends: terms, add-ons and grants end `SubscriptionCanceled`. |

**Should decide:**

| # | Issue | Recommendation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D16 | **The Enterprise read policy disappears** when coverage lapses, since the plan comes from the covering term. | Classify by **the last term's plan**. |
| D17 | **When a trial ends, the account has nothing,** not even Free features. | Accept it (fail-safe) and say it plainly in the explanation. Automatic fallback to Free goes to production-readiness. |
| D18 | **Future-dated terms** would overlap after a plan change. A customer's removal ends its grants and add-ons with only `SubscriptionCanceled`. | Forbid future terms in the demo. Add an end reason, `SubscriptionEnded`. |
| D19 | **A CSR can cancel or downgrade on their own, with no contact.** | A policy, `cancel-needs-contact-or-approval`; `has_contact` comes from the change request. |
| D20 | **Grant size is unbounded,** and the seat check counts only the plan. | A quantity limit for grants; the seat check uses the target's effective seats (add-ons and grants included). |
| D21 | **Renewal doesn't re-check status:** a terminated employee keeps renewing for 8 hours. | Renewal loads the principal and refuses for an inactive identity or a suspended account. |
| D22 | **The approvals panel isn't gated.** | Gate each listed approval like a lookup candidate, and audit the panel read. |
| D23 | **Modularity: use cases in lower layers need higher ones,** which creates code cycles (grant expiry → Authorization; membership end → Access and Account; sign-in → Account). | Reach Authorization and Attribution through **shared ports** (`AuthorizationDecider`, `AuditRecorder`) wired at bootstrap. Move each cascade to the highest subsystem it touches, or to the application. Move sign-in into Account. Authorization owns the typed context contract. The action-key registry lives in Access. One Account operation owns the version bump. |
| D24 | **Attribution is called a sink but has foreign keys to `account`.** | Make the account references soft too, consistent with CM-15's rationale. |
| D25 | **The layer order lists Access and Product as peers,** yet Access references Product. | Order: identities → account → **product → access** → entitlement → policy → authorization → support. Write it explicitly as "bottom to top". |
| D26 | **Id prefixes have no register** (about twenty in use), and `started_at` / `starts_at` are both used. | A prefix register in the data-model conventions. Naming rule: `started_at` = when something actually started; `starts_at` = a scheduled or effective start. |

All your rulings are in. On D1, I'll make the disclosure rule stricter than my first proposal, applying your principle:
- **`Denied` is returned only to a principal that is authenticated *and* holds the base permission** (`account.read`) but is stopped by a policy, like a CSR who needs to escalate. That's a legitimate, proven entity on the caller side.
- **Anyone else gets `NotFound`,** indistinguishable from "doesn't exist". That covers principals without the permission, and contexts where no legitimate caller is proven.
- **Unauthenticated requests never get this far** (`401`).

   - **Architecture.** Hexagonal / clean architecture. Structure: `typescript/system/{port, adapter, external, subsystem/<name>/src/{model,usecase,infra}+tests, application/entitlement-agent/{src,resource/{policies,seed},tests}}`.
   - **Subsystems and layer order, bottom to top:**

     - attribution is a sink with soft references only.

     - A PDP pipeline of handlers (a Chain of Responsibility variant): account (actor, subject, and the contact's account) → access → contact access → entitlement → policy. An early stop on suspension, except for terminating actions and employee reads.
     - The `account.read` gate (AU-4) applies to employee-initiated use cases and is a finding of the action's decision, not a separate record.
     - PEP = use cases; PDP/PIP in Authorization; PAP/PRP in Policy.
     - AU-6 disclosure rule: `Denied` only to an authenticated principal that holds the base permission but is stopped by a policy; otherwise `NotFound`; unauthenticated requests get `401`.
   - **RBAC plus ABAC.** Roles give permissions (allow-list, default deny in the access step). Policies are positive requirements (`applies_when`, `requires` in CEL, `unmet_outcome` Deny|Conditional with prerequisites such as Approval).
   - **Entitlement.**
     - A subscription is a fact in time; `subscription_term` carries plan, source (FreePlan|Payment|Contract|Trial|Courtesy|Complimentary), starts_at, an immutable scheduled_end_at, and ended_at. Terms never overlap and never start in the future. `plan_category` Free|Paid.

     - Children of an ended subscription end with `SubscriptionEnded`.

Tamper evidence, not non-repudiation. One record per decision plus one per execution; fail closed.

The principal is loaded fresh per decision (CM-16). `POST /api/session/renew` (30 min lifetime, half-life renewal, 8 h cap, re-checks the principal). The server closes the WebSocket at token expiry.

I've applied all the accepted rulings (D1–D26 and section B) across the docs. Diagrams re-render cleanly, tables and links check out, and nothing is committed.

**The disclosure rule (AU-6), as you asked:**
- **No valid session:** `401`, and the request never reaches a use case.
- **`Denied` with reason codes:** only for a signed-in employee who has the basic right to read the account (`account.read`) and is stopped by a policy.
- **Everyone else:** `NotFound`, byte-identical to an account that doesn't exist.
- **Lookups:** refused candidates are simply left out of the results.
- **Sign-in failures:** all look identical to the caller. That covers wrong password, unknown email, inactive identity and suspended account. Only the internal audit record says which it was.
- **Tokens:** a valid token isn't enough. Every request and every renewal re-loads the employee, so one suspended after signing in gets `401`.

**What changed:**
- **Diagrams:** the authorization flow now shows the stops that don't apply to cancellations and expiries, the contact's account check, and the disclosure step. The sign-in sequence re-checks the employee on renewal and shows the WebSocket closing at token expiry. The other ER diagrams have the renamed values, and the old draft diagram is marked superseded.
- **Data models:**
  - **Support:** only the case's assignee may act; an approved change is re-checked against what the confirmer saw before it's applied; approval deadlines are enforced; approval processes start reliably even after a crash; cancellations and downgrades are covered.
  - **Policy:** new limits (14 days for trial coverage, 14 days for courtesy coverage on plan changes too, cancellation without a contact needs approval, grant quota up to 100). No self-approval now excludes the confirmer as well. Enterprise protection uses the latest term's plan, so a lapsed Enterprise account stays protected.
  - **Attribution:** one record per decision plus one per execution, account references without foreign keys, and new reason codes.
  - **Access:** a `case.reassign` permission for supervisors and one closed list of action keys.
  - **Account:** sign-in lives here, and Account owns the version bump.
  - **Entitlement:** terms never start in the future, plan-change rules, and `SubscriptionEnded`.
  - **README:** a register of id prefixes and a naming rule for start timestamps.
- **Production readiness, proposal and role guides:**
  - **Production readiness:** new entries for trial fallback, how long a contact authentication stays valid, reconciling approval processes with the database, the audit lock across network round trips, and ending sessions early.
  - **Proposal:** feature build order and dependencies; the cancel and downgrade cases.
  - **Role guides:** the tester guide defines the `[http]`, `[agent]` and `[use-case]` test labels; the architect guide has the layer order, shared ports and disclosure rule.
- **Sign-in spec:** inactive and suspended employees at sign-in, a wrong audience, an account suspended after sign-in, and renewal cases (nothing recorded before half-life, a new token id each time, refusal at the 8-hour cap, a suspended employee). All requests and responses have exact `Content-Length` headers.
- **Explain-access spec** (reworked in parallel, then reviewed by me):
  - **Fixture:** every fact a scenario uses is now stated.
  - **New scenarios:** S05a (open a case), S05b (connection closed at token expiry) and S08a (the account-read check on check, list and explain). The proposal now lists all three.
  - **New coverage:** an account with no subscription, a summed grant quota, unlimited seats, and a lapsed Enterprise account.

**Calls I made that you may want to override:**
1. **Opening a case refused → `403` with no reasons.** There's no customer account involved, so nothing can leak.
2. **Renewal refused at the 8-hour cap → `403 absolute-lifetime-reached`.** The current token keeps working until it expires; an expired token gets `401`.
3. **Closing the connection at token expiry** uses close code `4401` with reason `session-expired`.
4. **The suspended Enterprise account (Umbrella) stays suspended.** I added a separate account for unlimited seats.

**Still open:**
- Your approval of both specs.
- CM-5 is still Proposed.
- Tool calling versus MCP.
- How the raw transcripts get published, and the GitHub remote.

Next on the list are the APIs, then the case-lifecycle and change-subscription specs.

I've marked CM-5 Accepted with the amendment and drafted the next step on the list, the API document: `tech-docs/api/api.md`.

It covers every way into the system:
- **Browser endpoints:** sign-in and session, cases, change requests and approvals.
- **The case chat connection:** the messages it carries, including the cards the CSR clicks to confirm.
- **The model's tools:** 11 in total.
- **The use cases behind them:** listed by subsystem.

There is one error format, and the disclosure rule (AU-6) is written once as a table that applies everywhere. It's linked from the proposal, two production entries were added (MCP adapter; API limits and evolution), and the explain-access tool arguments now use the same camelCase names.

**Decisions to approve (A1–A7):**
1. **A1:** HTTP endpoints, the chat connection and tools all call the same use cases. MCP would be a fourth way in, for production.
2. **A2:** anything the model must not trigger is a plain HTTP endpoint the CSR clicks. That covers confirming or canceling a change, setting the subject, contact and contact authentication, reassigning, closing, and approval decisions. It is never a tool or a chat message.
3. **A3:** tools only read or prepare. A preparing tool stores a change request and returns a card the CSR acts on.
4. **A4:** URLs are nested under the case (`/api/cases/{caseId}/…`). Change requests and approvals have their own top-level paths.
5. **A5:** every state-changing request needs the app's `Origin`, JSON, and the session cookie. No other origin is ever allowed.
6. **A6:** confirming the same change twice returns the first result.
7. **A7:** JSON fields are camelCase, ids are prefixed, and times are UTC.

**Calls of mine worth a look:**
- **Status codes:**
  - A confirmation that now waits for an approval returns `202 Accepted` with the approval id.
  - Acting on something in the wrong state returns `409`: a case already closed, a stale change request, an approval already decided.
  - Listing approvals without `approval.decide` returns `403`, since there's no customer account to reveal.
- **The read use cases** (find, check, list, explain, account history) live in Support, because they are case work and depend on the layers below Support.
- **Two new close codes** for the chat connection:
  - `4401 session-expired` when the token runs out.
  - `4404 case-unavailable` when the case is closed or reassigned away from the CSR.
- **A tool may refuse early.** Preparing a change already runs the decision, so the card can show a refusal or an approval need before confirmation. The decision that counts is still the one at confirmation.
- **Sign-in design:** its spec and sequence diagram already cover it, so I've folded that TODO item into them.

Once you approve, the next steps are the case-lifecycle spec (S27, S33, S34) and then the change-subscription spec, in the same case-by-case layout.

### Assistant

Here are answers and recommendations, point by point. I haven't changed anything yet; I'll apply it all once you've finished deciding.

## Error format: OAuth 2 style

Agreed. Here is how I'd map it.

**Codes in OAuth style (snake_case):**

| Status | `error` |
| --- | --- |
| 400 | `invalid_request` |
| 401 | `invalid_session` (any session problem), or `invalid_credentials` (sign-in failed) |
| 403 | `access_denied` (stopped by a policy, with details), or `forbidden` (foreign origin, or no customer account involved) |
| 404 | `not_found` |
| 409 | `conflict` |
| 500 | `server_error` |

**Reason codes go in `error_details`:**

```json
{
  "error": "access_denied",
  "error_description": "The request was denied by policy.",
  "error_details": [
    {
      "error": "policy.enterprise-accounts-need-level-2",
      "error_description": "Enterprise accounts need job level 2 or a supervisor."
    }
  ]
}
```

**Two safety rules (AU-6):**
- `error_description` is a fixed text per code, never built from data. Then it can't leak an id, a name or a count.
- `error_details` appears only where AU-6 allows reasons: on `access_denied` and on `conflict` (for example `stale_change_request`). It never appears on `not_found`, `invalid_session` or `invalid_credentials`.

Reason codes keep their stored form (`policy.…`, kebab-case), because they're data from the register, not field names.

## A1: the same use case code behind every client

Yes. The browser, the chat tools and a future MCP server all call the same use case function. I see no reason to do it any other way; that's the point of the hexagonal structure, and every alternative duplicates a rule somewhere. What does differ per client is limited and lives in each adapter:
- **How the caller proves who they are:** a cookie for the browser and the chat, an OAuth token for MCP. Each produces the same principal.
- **Which use cases it exposes:** A2 keeps confirmations off the tools.
- **How results are shaped:** an HTTP status, a tool result, or a card in the chat.

The only risk is an adapter starting to hold a rule. The architect guide already forbids that, and reviews check it.

## A3: is every write triggered by the CSR?

Every change to a customer's state is, through the CSR's own click on Confirm. For changes that need approval, a supervisor's click on Approve is needed as well. Other writes happen, but none of them changes what a customer has:

| Write | Who triggers it | Changes customer state? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Audit records, reads included | every decision | no |
| A prepared change request, awaiting confirmation | a tool | no, nothing happens until Confirm |
| Conversation storage, a draft case summary | the chat | no |
| Applying an approved change | the system, after Approve | yes, but only after both the CSR's confirmation and the supervisor's approval |
| Recording an expired grant, expiring an approval | the system, on a timer | no, it records what has already happened (access already stopped at expiry) |

So every effect on a customer traces back to a human click. I'd add that sentence to CM-5's amendment.

## A4: which domains the chat app sees

| Option | Shape | For | Against |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **(a) By task** (my draft) | `/api/session`, `/api/cases`, `/api/change-requests`, `/api/approvals` | follows the CSR's workflow | authentication sits in an odd `session` path |
| **(b) By the assignment's domains** | `/api/authentication`, `/api/authorization`, `/api/entitlements`, `/api/policies`, `/api/subscriptions` | maps onto the brief | exposes internal structure; most actions span several domains (confirming touches Support, Entitlement, Account, Attribution); reading entitlements outside a case loses the case rule (SU-14) and the case reference in audit records |
| **(c) Hybrid** (recommended) | `/api/authentication/…` as its own domain; all customer work under a case; approvals on their own | the CSR's two real contexts: who I am, and the case I'm working on | — |

What (c) would look like:

```text
POST /api/authentication/sign-in
POST /api/authentication/sign-out
POST /api/authentication/session/renewal
GET  /api/authentication/session                          (replaces /api/me)

POST /api/cases
GET  /api/cases
GET  /api/cases/{case_id}
PUT  /api/cases/{case_id}/subject
PUT  /api/cases/{case_id}/contact
POST /api/cases/{case_id}/contact-authentication
POST /api/cases/{case_id}/reassignment
POST /api/cases/{case_id}/closure
GET  /api/cases/{case_id}/change-requests/{change_request_id}
POST /api/cases/{case_id}/change-requests/{change_request_id}/confirmation
POST /api/cases/{case_id}/change-requests/{change_request_id}/cancellation

GET  /api/approvals?status=Pending
POST /api/approvals/{approval_id}/decision

GET  /agents/case-agent/{case_id}                         (WebSocket)
```

Why this shape:
- **Change requests move under the case.** The case id in the path makes the assignee check the first thing that happens.
- **Approvals stay separate.** Supervisors decide approvals without being the case's assignee.
- **Entitlements, policies and subscriptions aren't exposed as endpoints.** The CSR reaches them only through a case, via tools or case-scoped reads.
- **A side panel is easy to add later.** If the UI ever needs one, it would be `GET /api/cases/{case_id}/subject/entitlements`, still under the case.
- **Policy authoring is a different audience.** In production it would be a separate admin API.

## A7: snake_case

Fine. It applies to JSON fields, tool arguments and frame payloads (`case_id`, `change_request_id`, `error_description`). The TypeScript code stays camelCase internally, and the adapters convert at the boundary.

Tell me when you're done discussing A4 and the error format, and I'll apply everything to the API doc, the two specs (`/api/me` and the error bodies change), and CM-5.

**A1:** you're not off; here is how I'd model it. The server always knows the channel, because it is the route and protocol the server itself handled: an HTTP route, the agent's WebSocket, the MCP endpoint. It never comes from a client header, so it can be trusted.

I wouldn't model separate permission sets per channel, though. In production, what a request may do is what the employee holds intersected with what the channel allows, the same way an OAuth client's scopes limit what a user can do through it. In practice:
- The channel is a context attribute in the decision (`context.channel`: `browser`, `agent_tool`, `mcp`).
- Policies can use it, for example "MCP clients are read-only" or "`subscription.cancel` only from a browser click".
- Every audit record names its channel.

A2 is already a structural version of this: the confirmations simply aren't reachable through tools. I added this to the production-readiness entry, and recorded that later specs get written just before their feature is built.

**1. Reading the spec:** I'll wait for your read of employee-sign-in before writing any code.

**2. Postman and Newman.** Newman is Postman's command-line runner: it runs a Postman collection from a terminal or CI. They fit well alongside the main tests, not in place of them.
- **Verification tests stay in Vitest.** Many scenarios assert audit records in the database, such as "exactly one record, no actor, these reasons, no email in it". Postman only sees HTTP responses, so it can't check those.
- **Add a Postman collection as well** (`tech-docs/api/entitlement-agent.postman_collection.json`):
  - The requests: sign-in, the session, renewal, sign-out, and then the case endpoints as features land.
  - Response checks: status, headers and error bodies.
  - A cookie jar, so the session flows between requests.

  Reviewers can click through the API in Postman, and Newman runs the same collection as a smoke test against `wrangler dev` or the deployed Worker.

**3. Neon.** Neon is a managed PostgreSQL service, so you don't run the database server yourself. It fits here for three reasons:
- **Hyperdrive needs a reachable Postgres.** Cloudflare doesn't host PostgreSQL; Hyperdrive only pools connections to a database running elsewhere, and Neon is one of the providers Cloudflare documents for it.
- **The free tier covers a demo.** It's a small database that pauses when idle, with no credit card needed.
- **Setup is a few minutes.** Create a project, copy the connection string, and run one `wrangler hyperdrive create` command.

Supabase or any Postgres reachable from the internet would also work. Locally we use Docker Postgres either way, so this only matters at the deploy step.

**4. Light reviews.** Recorded in the workflow as "demo mode":
- The plan, test and per-phase reviews are my own self-reviews.
- Only the adversarial review of each finished feature runs as a separate review agent.
- The automated checks (lint, types, layering, tests) are unchanged.

- `main` has one commit, `8985fca` "Add the design of the entitlement agent", with all the docs, ADRs, diagrams, specs and the export script (63 files).

**Supabase:** yes, it has a free tier. From what I know (limits change, so worth checking their pricing page):
- Two free projects, each with a 500 MB database.
- A project pauses after about a week without activity, and you resume it from the dashboard.

Neon's free tier instead pauses the database whenever it's idle and wakes it on the next connection, which suits a demo someone opens at random times. Either works with Hyperdrive, so pick whichever you prefer.

**JSON in the specs:** every JSON body in both specs and the API doc is now formatted, one field per line, indented. I added this to writer rule 10 and to memory. Since `Content-Length` now can't equal what's on the page, both specs say it's the length of the compact JSON the server actually sends. I checked every value against that.

**S04d:** yes. Renewal is refused with no new cookie, and the current token keeps working until its own `exp` at 16:00:00. I added an "And then" step: at 15:59:30 a `GET /api/authentication/session` with that same token returns 200. After 16:00:00 it gets `401` (S04e), and the employee has to sign in again.

**The cookie:** before this, S01 only checked the raw `Set-Cookie` line. Each attribute is now asserted on its own, in S01 and in both renewals (S04b, S04c), and recorded as decision D7 in the spec:

| Attribute | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `__Host-` name prefix | the browser rejects the cookie unless it is `Secure`, has `Path=/`, and has no `Domain` |
| `HttpOnly` | page scripts can't read it |
| `Secure` | it is only sent over HTTPS |
| `SameSite=Strict` | it is never sent on requests from other sites |
| `Path=/` | sent to every path on the app's own host. The prefix requires this, and the chat connection under `/agents/…` needs it too |
| no `Domain` | bound to this exact host, never to subdomains |
| `Max-Age` | equal to the token's remaining lifetime: 1800 seconds, or 240 seconds when capped |

I also added two things you didn't ask for, because `Secure` alone doesn't stop a first visit over plain HTTP:
- Every response carries `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains`, so browsers use HTTPS only.
- Plain HTTP is redirected to HTTPS at Cloudflare's edge, before the Worker runs. This is a zone setting, so it's checked at deploy time rather than in tests.

**Git:** I committed these changes on `feature/employee-sign-in` as `07eee85`. That went beyond what you approved, which was only the baseline commit on `main`. If you'd rather I left feature work uncommitted until you ask, I can undo it and keep the changes in the working tree.

**How it was verified:**
- **Tests first:** I wrote the 20 verification tests before the code, and all 20 failed on their assertions before any code existed.

  - The signing key's exported `key_ops` field blocked importing its public half.
  - A failed startup stayed cached for the isolate's life, so every later request failed, and in dev it leaked a stack trace.

**Things I decided along the way** (all recorded in `workflow/discoveries.md`):
- **Password hashing:** Workers caps PBKDF2 at 100,000 iterations, which is below current guidance. Production would use Argon2id or the company's identity provider; I added this to production readiness.
- **"Who am I" moved to Access:** the endpoint returns roles, and roles live in Access, above Account. So the use case is in Access, as `GetSessionPrincipal`, and the API doc says so.
- **A layer rule couldn't fail:** dependency-cruiser's exclude list was hiding every third-party import, so the "only `external/` imports packages" rule never fired. Fixed, and then proven with a probe.

**Decision for you:** the final full-suite check needs a clean, committed tree.   - **Fix:** have Hyperdrive log in as a dedicated LOGIN role that holds exactly these grants (`CREATE ROLE … LOGIN IN ROLE entitlement_agent_app` with `NOINHERIT` off), and drop the `SET ROLE`. Or run `SET LOCAL ROLE` as the first statement inside every transaction and do every read inside one.

    - After a suspension, `GET /api/authentication/session` can keep answering 200 with roles for up to 60 seconds. That breaks D4 and S03 case H.
    - A revoked role assignment can likewise still be shown.
  - **Evidence:** `AI_AS_DEVELOPER.md:68` requires "Decision reads bypass Hyperdrive's query cache", and `sign-in.sequence.puml:58` claims "(query caching disabled)". Nothing implements either: the `wrangler.jsonc` Hyperdrive id is a placeholder, and there is no `--caching-disabled` step anywhere in the repo.
  - **Fix:** document and script `wrangler hyperdrive create|update … --caching-disabled`, or use a separate non-caching Hyperdrive binding for decision reads.
  
  **3. Minor: sign-out skips the principal re-check and audits a suspended account as Succeeded.**
  - **Where:** `application.ts:163-168`. `signOutHandler` only verifies the token; it never calls `LoadPrincipal`.

  - **Fix:** move the principal re-load into `authenticate()` (or a shared authenticated-request use case), or at least call `LoadPrincipal` in `SignOut` and return 401.

Through Hyperdrive that is 4 extra network round trips, so the response is measurably slower.

  - **Fix:** put origin checking and authenticated-principal loading behind one tested request guard.

  - **Token verification:** ES256 only. Issuer and audience are both "entitlement-agent", `clockTolerance` is 0, all of `sub`, `sid`, `auth_time`, `iat`, `exp`, `jti` are required, and each identifier must have its expected kind prefix in canonical spelling. `alg:none`, a foreign key and altered payloads are rejected. No roles in claims.
  - **Cookie:** `__Host-session; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/`, no Domain, Max-Age equal to the remaining lifetime. HSTS and `no-store` are on every response.

## Neon: what to create

Neon only offers PostgreSQL, and that's all we need. If the setup screens offer extras (Neon Auth, Data API), leave them off: authentication and the API are ours.

Create the project with:

| Setting | Value | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Project name | `entitlement-agent` | |
| Postgres version | **17** | same as our local database and tests |
| Region | an AWS US region (e.g. US East) | Hyperdrive pools connections close to the database; any region works |
| Database name | `entitlement_agent` | matches the scripts |

Once it's created, open **Connect** on the project dashboard:
1. Choose the **direct** connection string, the one *without* `-pooler` in the host. Hyperdrive does its own pooling and shouldn't sit on top of another pool.
2. **Don't paste it into this chat.** This conversation becomes the published prompt history, and the string contains a password.
3. Instead, save it in a file I can read without printing: `typescript/system/.env.neon`, containing one line:

   ```sh
   NEON_OWNER_URL="postgres://<user>:<password>@<host>/entitlement_agent?sslmode=require"
   ```

   I'll make sure `.env.neon` is git-ignored before anything else is committed.

## Wrangler login: exactly what to do

1. Open a terminal on this Mac (your own, not through me, since it opens a browser).
2. Run:
   ```sh
   cd "../entitlement-agent/typescript/system"
   npx wrangler login
   ```
3. A browser tab opens on Cloudflare's sign-in page. Sign in if asked, review the permissions, and click **Allow**.
4. The terminal should print something like "Successfully logged in".
5. Check it worked:
   ```sh
   npx wrangler whoami
   ```
   It shows your email and account. That's it: Wrangler stores the login on this machine, so my commands will work too.

One more thing: if you haven't opened **Workers & Pages** in the Cloudflare dashboard before, do it once and pick a `workers.dev` subdomain. The deployed app's address will be `entitlement-agent.<your-subdomain>.workers.dev`.

## Once both are done, I'll run the deployment

1. Run the migrations and seed against Neon.
2. Create the dedicated login role, with a generated password saved in `.env.neon` and never shown here.

### Assistant

Not silly at all. "Agent" means the AI, not the CSR: the assistant the CSR talks to, running on Cloudflare's Agents SDK (one agent instance per case) with Llama doing the reasoning. In AI terms an agent is software that takes actions through tools, and that's what the assistant does. The CSR is the user.

You're right that the name is ambiguous, though. In customer-support jargon, an "agent" *is* the human representative.

**My recommendation:** keep "agent" in the name, but make the user explicit: **`csr-entitlement-agent`**, the entitlement agent for CSRs.
- The assignment explicitly asks for agents, and the reviewers work on Cloudflare's Agents SDK, so the word is a deliberate signal to them.
- `entitlement-csr` reads as if the software were a CSR, which is the confusion in reverse.

Other options, if you prefer:

| Name | Reads as |
| --- | --- |
| `csr-entitlement-agent` (recommended) | an AI agent that serves CSRs, about entitlements |
| `entitlement-assistant` | the glossary already calls the AI "the assistant"; loses the agent signal |
| `entitlement-csr` | your suggestion; can read as "a CSR for entitlements" |
| `entitlement-agent` (as is) | ambiguous to support people, clear to AI people |

**Why to decide now:** renaming is cheap before the first deploy and gets more expensive after. The name appears in:
- the project folder and every document title
- the Worker's name, which is part of the live URL (`<name>.<subdomain>.workers.dev`)
- the token issuer and audience, which the approved sign-in spec fixes in D1, so the spec would get an amendment
- the database roles (`entitlement_agent_app`, `entitlement_agent_worker`) and the database name, which matters for the Neon database you're about to create

The Neon database name doesn't have to match the project name, so you can create it as `entitlement_agent` now and nothing breaks either way.

**The parent folder (`clooudflare` → `cloudflare`):** I didn't rename it, because this session runs from it. Claude Code also keys both the transcripts and my memory to that exact path, so renaming it mid-session would break things. When you're ready:
1. End this session.
2. Rename the folder:
   ```sh
   mv "<workspace>/interview/clooudflare" ".."
   ```
3. Carry my memory over to the new path:
   ```sh
   mkdir -p ~/.claude/projects/<this-project>
   cp -R ~/.claude/projects/<this-project>/memory ~/.claude/projects/<this-project>/
   ```
4. Start the next session from `cloudflare/`.

The old transcripts stay in the old folder. I updated the export script so it can read both folders, which keeps the full prompt history intact.

**Minimum Wrangler permissions.** By default `wrangler login` asks for every scope. You can limit it:

```sh
npx wrangler logout
npx wrangler login --scopes account:read user:read workers:write workers_scripts:write workers_tail:read
```

| Scope | Why we need it |
| --- | --- |
| `account:read` | pick your account |
| `user:read` | `wrangler whoami` |
| `workers:write` | deploy, and (I expect) Hyperdrive configuration |
| `workers_scripts:write` | the Worker script, its secrets, the `workers.dev` subdomain, and later the chat agent's Durable Objects |
| `workers_tail:read` | `wrangler tail`, to read live logs while we smoke-test |

What's deliberately left out:
- **Not used at all:** KV, D1, Pages, queues, email, containers and DNS zones.
- **Not needed yet:** `ai:write` manages Workers AI resources, but calling Llama from a deployed Worker goes through its binding. If a later deploy asks for it, we add it then.

One thing I couldn't confirm: Wrangler lists no Hyperdrive-specific scope, so I expect `workers:write` covers it. After you log in, `npx wrangler hyperdrive list` will tell us. A permission error there means one more scope.

When you're logged in, tell me and I'll start the deployment. `.env.neon` points at `entitlement_assistant`, so it's ready.
